--- Page 219 ---
<meta>This page follows the index of fugues and serves as a formal list of errata (corrections) for the musical portions of the book. The author, Michael Maier, expresses regret for the printing errors that occurred while he was away from the press.</meta>

# ADMONITION
## TO THE MUSIC-LOVER.

**I** indeed desired previously to act as *Prometheus* <note>Greek: "Forethought," the one who thinks ahead</note> rather than *Epimetheus* <note>Greek: "Afterthought," the one who thinks after the fact</note>; and therefore I wrote and revised everything concerning those musical fugues <note>Latin: *fugas musicales*</note> with the greatest diligence. But well-deliberated plans sometimes turn out poorly: and thus while I was away, many notes of these fugues, I know not how, were transposed and not printed as correctly as one would wish. Therefore, I am compelled to place here a corrective admonition, especially for the preceding songs <note>Latin: *cantilenarum*</note>. Anyone skilled in music will easily notice, even without my warning, that the individual fugues must be consistent in all respects—in the values, the ascent, and the descent of the individual notes—except that one voice follows another at different pitches <note>Latin: *clavibus*, literally "keys" or "clefs"</note>. Hence, the reader himself will be able to correct a typographical error if anything occurs elsewhere that is placed other than correctly. In the meantime, I will set down here some faults <note>Latin: *mendas*</note> as examples, which I noted while scanning briefly (though it is outside the usual manner of music):

*In Fugue 2, page 16, line 3: the fourth note should be a quarter note <note>Latin: *semiminima*</note>, not on pitch A, but on D, above the text "Romulus."*
*In Fugue 6, page 32, line 1: the sixth note should be a whole note <note>Latin: *semibrevis*</note> on pitch E, above the text "pingui" <note>Latin: "rich" or "thick"</note>.*
*In Fugue 9, page 44, line 1: the 19th note should be a whole note on pitch F, above the text "aurea" <note>Latin: "golden"</note>.*
*In the same fugue, line 3: note 15, a quarter note on pitch A, should be completely deleted as superfluous.*
*In Fugue 11, page 52, line 1: the fifth note on pitch G should not be a whole note, but a half note <note>Latin: *minima*</note>, above the text "sobolem" <note>Latin: "offspring"</note>.*
*In the same place, line 2: note 2 on pitch G should not be a half note, but a whole note, above the text "fert" <note>Latin: "brings" or "carries"</note>.*
*In Fugue 12, page 56, line 3: note 13 on pitch B should be an eighth note <note>Latin: *fusa*</note> with a dot on the preceding quarter note, above the text "Helicona" <note>referring to Mount Helicon, home of the Muses</note>.*
*In the same, line 4: note 15 should not be a quarter note, but a half note, on pitch D, above the text "petendus" <note>Latin: "to be sought"</note>.*
*In Fugue 13, page 60, line 4: the first note should be a half note on pitch E, at the text "Inde" <note>Latin: "thence"</note>.*
*Fugue 14, page 64, line 2: notes 6 and 7 should be eighth notes on pitches B and A, above the text "humana" <note>Latin: "human"</note>.*
*In the same place, line 6: the clef B at the beginning is transposed.*
*Fugue 16, page 72, line 4: note 4 on A should be a quarter note.*
*Fugue 17, page 76, line 14: on F there should be a half note, not a whole note, above the text "ignis" <note>Latin: "fire"</note>.*
*Fugue 19, page 84, line 1: note 14 should be a half note, not on C, but on D, above the text "ordine" <note>Latin: "in order"</note>.*
*In the same, line 2: from the beginning it should be thus:* <image-desc>A short musical staff notation snippet containing four notes: a semibreve on the C ledger line below the staff, followed by two minims on the D space below the staff, and ending with another semibreve on the C ledger line.</image-desc> *above the text "terra quorum" <note>Latin: "of whose earth"</note>, for the first note should be a whole note on C, the second and third should be half notes on D, and the fourth a whole note on C.*
*In the same, line 4: the second note should be a whole note not on G, but on E.*
*In Fugue 22, page 96, line 4: note 1 should be a half note on B, at the text "Saturni" <note>Latin: "of Saturn"</note>.*
*In Fugue 23, page 100, line 1: notes 23 and 24 on D and C should be eighth notes, at the text "fecit" <note>Latin: "he made"</note>.*
*In the same, line 3: note 7 should be a dotted half note on C, at the text "mira" <note>Latin: "wondrous things"</note>.*
*In Fugue 24, page 104, line 1: note 13 should not be a whole note on E, but on G, at the text "cura" <note>Latin: "care"</note>.*
*In Fugue 25, page 108, line 4: note 5 on pitch B should not be a quarter note, but a half note, at the text "funere" <note>Latin: "at the funeral" or "death"</note>.*
*In Fugue 26, page 112, line 3: note 12 on F should be an eighth note.*

<summary>In this errata page, the author apologizes for musical printing errors that occurred during his absence and provides a detailed list of corrections for specific notes and pitches in the fugues.</summary>
<keywords>errata, music theory, fugues, printing errors, Michael Maier, Prometheus, Epimetheus, musical notation, canons</keywords>

--- Page 220 ---
<meta>This page continues the list of musical errata (corrections) from the previous section. The author, Michael Maier, is providing specific instructions to correct the musical notation for Fugues 27 through 30, before offering a general apology for any remaining errors in the rest of the book.</meta>

In Fugue 27, page 116, line 3: the rests at the beginning should have the value of two half notes <note>Latin: *minimarum*</note>, or two half-beats <note>original: *duo dimidii tactus*; the *tactus* was the basic rhythmic pulse in Renaissance and Baroque music</note>.
And the second-to-last note on pitch A should not be a whole note <note>Latin: *semibrevis*</note>, but a half note <note>Latin: *minima*</note>, at the text "rose garden" <note>original: *rosetum*</note>.
In Fugue 28, page 120, line 1: the 15th note immediately before the rest should be a half note <note>Latin: *minima*</note> on pitch E, at the text "they shine" <note>original: *fulgent*</note>.
In Fugue 29, page 124, line 1: the last two quarter notes <note>Latin: *semiminimae*</note> should be placed on pitches D and F, at the text "more powerful" <note>original: *potentior*</note>.
In the same place, on line 3, after the 12th note, a whole note is missing: a whole note <note>Latin: *semibrevis*</note> on pitch G at the text "Salamander" <note>original: *Salamandra*; a central figure in alchemical lore, said to survive in the heart of the fire</note>.
In Fugue 30, page 128, line 1: the 11th note should be a quarter note <note>Latin: *semiminima*</note> on F, not on D, at the text "you drive" <note>original: *agis*</note>. In the same place: after the 19th note, delete the dot.

In the remaining twenty fugues that follow, as well as in all other places where any errors might occur—whether in the musical notes, the texts, or the discourses <note>Latin: *discursibus*; these are the scholarly and philosophical essays that accompany each emblem in the book</note>—besides those already mentioned, let the honest reader and singer attribute them not to us, but to some misfortune brought upon us from I know not where, and may they kindly correct the errors themselves.

## Front Cover of the Volume

->[Front Cover]<-

This volume is bound in a luxurious **dark green leather**, specifically <term>morocco leather: A high-quality, durable goatskin leather known for its visible grain, often used in fine bookbinding.</term> 

The cover is decorated with an elaborate <term>gilt: A decorative finish achieved by applying gold leaf to the surface.</term> border. This design was created through <term>gold tooling: A technique where heated metal stamps or rollers are used to press gold leaf into the leather, creating permanent, shimmering patterns.</term> 

The ornamentation consists of several distinct layers:
*   A wide outer frame featuring intricate, repeating **floral and leaf-like motifs**.
*   A set of thin, delicate gold lines that define the boundaries of the frame.
*   A secondary inner border composed of a single, precise row of small gold dots.

The central area of the cover is left plain, allowing the natural texture of the leather to remain visible. A portion of the **spine** can be seen along the edge, which also features decorative gold-work to match the front.

<summary>This page describes the exterior of the manuscript, a fine example of luxury bookbinding featuring dark green morocco leather and intricate gold-tooled floral borders.</summary>

# Binding and Spine

->**Spine Description**<-

The spine of the volume is crafted from the same dark green leather as the covers and is divided into several compartments by raised horizontal bands. These bands are decorated with gilded geometric patterns.

The compartments alternating between the text contain central octagonal floral rosettes, a common motif in early 17th-century luxury bookbinding. The title and the date of publication are stamped into the leather using gold leaf.
